# Sources, gaps and guesses

Where each calendar's days, feasts, colors, names, readings and artwork come from, and what is still missing or guessed. **Edit `docs/provenance.csv`, then run `python3 tools/provenance.py`**; this page is written from it. Where the calendar's data disagree with a source, the item waits in [provenance-review.md](provenance-review.md).

Each row of the CSV is one claim: `church` (one or more keys from `data/traditions.csv`, or `all`), `aspect`, `status`, `detail` (which part), `source` and `notes`. The status is one of:

- ✓ **sourced**: taken from the source named
- ◐ **partial**: from the source named, with known pieces missing
- ≈ **inferred**: worked out from a source's rules, or borrowed from a related church, rather than stated outright
- ? **uncited**: stated as fact, but no source is named yet
- ~ **guess**: a choice or assumption made without a source
- ✗ **missing**: not in the calendar yet

The comments at the head of each data file give the same sources in more detail; CHANGELOG.md says when each was added.

## By church and aspect

## Open items

Everything that is missing, guessed, uncited or partial, by aspect.

### Days

| Church | Status | What | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Folkekirken (Evangelical Lutheran Church in Denmark) | ✗ missing | Great Prayer Day (Bededag) | Left out; no longer a public holiday since 2024 |
| Divine Worship 2021 (Personal Ordinariates) | ◐ partial | Privileged weekdays: 17-24 December and the Christmas octave are not ranked (rank 9), so memorials there are not reduced to commemorations; a transferred Immaculate Conception (9 December) loses its obligation mark, though the Ordo keeps the obligation in the United States | Divine Worship: The Missal, Table of Liturgical Days no. 9; Ordo 2026 p. 3 note 2 |
| Eastern Orthodox, Ukrainian Greek Catholic Church | ◐ partial | Revised Julian calendar with Pascha by the Julian paschalion (Julian calendar optional for Old Calendar churches) | UGCC part confirmed: from 1 Sept 2023 the UGCC in Ukraine keeps fixed feasts on the 'new style' while keeping the current (Julian) Paschalia ('We have decided to leave Paschalia as it is today'); parishes could keep the Julian calendar until 2025 with their bishop's blessing. The UGCC calls this the 'Gregorian style'; it gives the same dates as the Revised Julian until 2800. The decision is for the UGCC in Ukraine; diaspora eparchies decide for themselves. Eastern Orthodox part (which churches use which calendar) not checked against a fetched source. |
| Eastern Orthodox, Ukrainian Greek Catholic Church, Byzantine Catholic Church (Ruthenian) | ◐ partial | Byzantine year structure: Indiction start 1 Sept, Sundays after Pentecost counted to Zacchaeus, Triodion, Pentecostarion | The 2026 MCI calendar lists 1 Sept as 'Beginning of the Church Year'; the Sundays before/after the Exaltation (13 and 20 Sept), the Fathers of the Seventh Council (11 Oct), the Forefathers (13 Dec), the Sunday before the Nativity (20 Dec), after the Nativity (27 Dec), before Theophany (4 Jan) and after Theophany (11 Jan); the Sundays after Pentecost numbered through 'Thirty-second Sunday after Pentecost; Sunday of Zacchaeus' (18 Jan = Pascha-77); All Saints (31 May = Pascha+56); St Philip's Fast 15 Nov-24 Dec and the Apostles' Fast to 28 June. All match the rules in data/Orthodox/days.csv and periods.csv for 2026. The Liturgical Year page names the Triodion and Pentecostarion as the books of the movable cycle. Ruthenian source only; no Orthodox (OCA/GOARCH) structure page was fetched, though the readings rows already check the Sunday sequence against the OCA lectionary. OCA daily pages confirm the edge cases. When 30 or 31 Dec is a Sunday (31 Dec 2023, 30 Dec 2018), the OCA calls it only 'Sunday after Nativity' and has no 'Sunday before Theophany' that year, which matches the repo's date ge 01-01 rule. When the Nativity falls on a Sunday (2022), the OCA has no Sunday after Nativity: 26 Dec 2022 takes its readings (Gal 1:11-19, Matt 2:13-23) under the Synaxis. Jan 1 2023 is 'Sunday before Theophany'. Sources: [oca.org/…](https://www.oca.org/readings/daily/2023/12/31) ; [oca.org/…](https://www.oca.org/readings/daily/2018/12/30) ; [oca.org/…](https://www.oca.org/readings/daily/2022/12/26) ; [oca.org/…](https://www.oca.org/readings/daily/2023/01/01) |
| Kościół Ewangelicko-Augsburski (Poland) | ◐ partial | Ash Wednesday as Dzień Pokuty i Modlitwy; no November day of repentance | A parish of the church calls Ash Wednesday "Dzień Pokuty i Modlitwy (tzw. Środa Popielcowa)", which opens Passiontide. No source was found stating that there is no November day of repentance. |
| Armenian Apostolic Church | ◐ partial | Year begins with Advent (Hisnak): the First Sunday of Advent is the Sunday nearest 25 November; the Advent fast and its fifty days begin the Monday after the Sunday nearest 18 November | The Prelacy's 2025 lectionary has 16 November 'Tenth Sunday of the Exaltation of the Holy Cross (Eve of the Fast of Advent)' and 23 November 'First Sunday of Advent', which is the repo's sun>=11-22. The Georgia diocese gives Advent from 'the Sunday nearest to the 25th of November'. Sydney says 'Advent begins on the day after the Sunday nearest November 18'. The Eastern Diocese calls Hisnag 'the 50-day period' before the Nativity, which only works from the 18 November start. So the repo's year start is right for the First Sunday of Advent, but the docs' 'fifty days from the Sunday nearest 25 November' is wrong. |
| Ethiopian and Eritrean Orthodox Tewahedo | ◐ partial | Ethiopian year from Enkutatash (1 Meskerem: 11 Sep, 12 Sep before a Gregorian leap year); Easter by the Alexandrian (Ammonian) reckoning; nine major feasts of the Lord, Meskel 17 Meskerem, Hidar Tsion 21 Hidar, Gizret 6 Tir, Timket 11 Tir, Kana 12 Tir, Megabit Meskel 10 Megabit, Debre Tabor 13 Nehase, Filseta 16 Nehase; fasts (Nineveh, Abiy Tsom, Apostles, Filseta, Tsome Nebiyat) | Checked: Enkutatash 11 Sep (12 Sep in 2019, matching the repo's leap rule); Meskel 27 Sep with Demera the day before; Hidar Tsion = Hidar 21 (30 Nov); Timket = Tir 11, Kana = Tir 12; Debre Tabor = Nehase 13; Easter by Ammonius; Lent starts on a Monday; Apostles' fast starts after Pentecost. Disagreement: Tsome Nebiyat starts 15 Hidar (24 Nov; 25 Nov in the year before a Gregorian leap year) and is 44 days, but the repo starts it at Genna-43 (25 Nov), a day late in most years. The church's own site gives Abiy Tsom as 56 days (repo: 55 from Monday, plus Zewerede Sunday = 56, so consistent if counted from the Sunday). Not found in a source: Rikbe Kahinat at Easter+24, Feast of the Apostles 5 Hamle (Coptic 5 Epip is verified, same calendar), Ethiopian Nineveh date (matches the Coptic one by the shared rule; unverified for Ethiopia directly). Of the nine minor feasts of the Lord, Gizret (Our Lord and Savior Jesus Christ's Circumcision is celebrated on Tir 6) and Megabit Meskel (Megabit 10 is the founding of the Holy Cross; St Helena found the Cross on Megabit 10 (March 19th)) are added; Lidete Simeon and the Advent Sundays Sibket, Birhan and Nolawi are still left out, as no source found gives their days. Genna is kept on 7 Jan (28 Tahsas in years after a leap year), which julian(12-25) handles correctly. |
| Malankara Orthodox Syrian Church | ◐ partial | Gregorian calendar adopted 1953 (Encyclical No. 620 of Patriarch Ignatius Aphrem I, Dec 1952) and Western Easter; Nativity fast of 25 days, 1 December to Christmas; Apostles' fast 16-29 June; St Mary's fast 1-15 August | Skaria says the Malankara Church "switched entirely to the Gregorian calendar in 1953," following Encyclical No. 620. No fetched source states the Malankara Orthodox Easter rule outright, which is why this stays partial. Indirect evidence: the UK-Europe diocese's Easter circular is dated 11 April 2020 (Western Easter was 12 April, Julian 19 April), and the Syriac Orthodox (Jacobite) cathedral in Bengaluru lists Easter 2026 on 5 April, the Western date (Julian is 12 April). Nativity fast: "this fast lasts for 25 days, from the first of December till the Christmas day" (KL, adapted from mosc.in; BLR and DXB agree). periods.csv runs it 1-24 Dec with the feast on the 25th, so the data and the docs are consistent. The 13-day Apostles' fast (16-29 Jun) and 15-day St Mary's fast (1-15 Aug) match periods.csv. calendar.mosc.in loaded but showed no dates for these. |
| Syriac Orthodox Church of Antioch, Malankara Orthodox Syrian Church | ◐ partial | West Syriac year from Qudosh Idto (Sunday 30 Oct-5 Nov, eight Sundays before the Nativity) and Hudosh Idto, five Sundays of annunciations (Zechariah, Mary, Visitation, Birth of John, Revelation to Joseph), Sunday before the Nativity, Nativity and Denho, Great Lent from Kothne through the healing Sundays to Hosanna, Resurrection, New Sunday, Pentecost and the Cross | SOR and WUS say the year begins on the last Sunday of October if it falls on the 30th or 31st, otherwise on the first Sunday of November. That is the same day as the repo rule sun<12-25-49. The Malankara diocese lists Koodhosh Eetho on 2 Nov and Hoodhosh Eetho on 9 Nov for 2025, which agrees. The Sunday sequence matches. Fasts: the Syriac Orthodox Church keeps the Apostles' fast for three days from 26 June and the Virgin's fast for five days from 10 August (Barsoum; BSM, a Syriac Orthodox cathedral in India; SOR). The Malankara Orthodox keep 16-29 June and 1-15 August (KL, adapted from mosc.in). periods.csv applies the Malankara dates to both variants, so it is wrong for 'syriac'. Possible movable/fixed mismatches: the NE American Malankara diocese lists Severus on 8 Feb, Barsaumo on 3 Feb and Ephrem on 24 Feb as fixed dates, while days.csv makes them movable (easter-66, easter+46, easter-43). See 4-discrepancies.md. |

### Feasts

| Church | Status | What | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| United Methodist Church | ✗ missing | Annual-conference special Sundays | Sundays each annual conference dates for itself are left out. |
| Armenian Apostolic Church | ✗ missing | Most saints' days | they move each year to a non-fast weekday, so are not listed |
| Ambrosian Rite (Archdiocese of Milan) | ◐ partial | Solemnities and feasts (17 days), and 26 saints' feasts with readings of their own (Apostles, Evangelists, St Lawrence, St Benedict ...) and the 16 December commemoration | Memorials and lesser saints not included. GCatholic's colors are a third-party reading of the calendar; it marks the Circumcision (1 January) red where this calendar has white from the Messale |
| BCP 1928 (Episcopal Church), REC 2005 (Reformed Episcopal Church) | ◐ partial | Only the holy days of the book (no lesser commemorations) | feasts.csv: "every holy day of the book" |
| Assyrian Church of the East | ◐ partial | Commemorations: Mary (first or second Friday after the Nativity; 15 May for the harvest; 15 Aug), St Thomas 3 Jul, Transfiguration 6 Aug; Peter and Paul on a Friday of Denha | Confirmed: Thomas 3 Jul, Transfiguration 6 Aug, Mary 15 May and 15 Aug. Differences: (1) Mary's post-Nativity commemoration falls on the first or second Friday after the Nativity (Andrious; 5 Jan in 2024), not on 26 Dec. (2) The ACOE calendar calls 15 May "Protectress of the Harvest," not "of the Seeds." No fetched source calls 15 Aug "of the Vines"; the calendar names it the Dormition. (3) Peter and Paul are kept on a Friday of Denha (19 Jan 2024; Maclean), not on 29 Jun. The 25 Mar Annunciation was not found in a fetched ACOE source. |
| Ukrainian Greek Catholic Church, Byzantine Catholic Church (Ruthenian) | ◐ partial | Greek Catholic additions: Holy Eucharist (Thursday after All Saints) and St Josaphat (12 Nov); UGCC confirmed, Ruthenian unconfirmed | Official UGCC: the Head of the UGCC preached on Thursday 11 June 2026 (Julian Pascha 12 Apr + 60) 'у свято Торжественного поклоніння пречистим Тайнам Тіла і Крові Господа нашого Ісуса Христа' (the Holy Eucharist feast). On 12 Nov 2023, after the calendar change, he addressed pilgrims 'on the day of his [Josaphat's] memory'. Parish sites: Holy Eucharist is kept on the 'Thursday after the Sunday of All Saints' (Thursday 19 June 2025 = Pascha+60), often transferred to the following Sunday; a forum post says the UGCC Particular Law lists it. St Josaphat: 'The actual feast ... takes place in 12 November' (UGCC eparchy, new calendar); before 2023 the UGCC kept 12 Nov on the Julian calendar (25 Nov civil). Ruthenian: the Archeparchy of Pittsburgh's 2026 calendar has no entry for either (nothing on Thursday 4 June or on 12 Nov 2026), so the repo's ruthenian variant is unconfirmed. Parish and forum sources only; no official UGCC calendar page was fetched. |
| Eastern Orthodox, Ukrainian Greek Catholic Church, Byzantine Catholic Church (Ruthenian) | ◐ partial | Fixed feasts and saints (Twelve Great Feasts, greater saints, other commemorations) | Spot-checked dates and names, which match: 1 Sept Church New Year and Simeon Stylites; 4 Dec Barbara and John of Damascus; 11 June Bartholomew and Barnabas; 31 Aug Placing of the Cincture. The Ruthenian 2026 calendar also matches the Exaltation (14 Sept), the Archangels (8 Nov), the Entry (21 Nov) and the Circumcision with St Basil (1 Jan). One difference: the OCA keeps Great Martyr Katherine on 24 Nov, but the repo has 11-25 (Greek usage). The remaining dates are standard Menaion dates but were not individually fetched. |
| Evangelical Lutheran Church in America (ELW) | ◐ partial | Lesser festivals and commemorations | The ELCA lists confirm the dates of the lesser festivals in feasts.csv, including Thomas on July 3 and Matthias on May 14 (transferred to May 15 in 2026), with their colors. The commemorations themselves were not checked against an ELCA list. Wikipedia's "Calendar of saints (Lutheran)" (last resort, [en.wikipedia.org/…](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calendar_of_saints_%28Lutheran%29)) agrees on the dates spot-checked. The one commemoration found that was added since 2006, the Emanuel Nine on June 17 (2019 Churchwide Assembly, adopted Aug 8, 2019), is missing from feasts.csv. The Emanuel Nine (June 17) has since been added. |
| Maronite Catholic Church | ◐ partial | Great feasts and saints (11 days, incl. All Saints 1 Nov, incl. St Maron 9 Feb, St Sharbel 3rd Sunday of July) | Catholic World Report: St Charbel's feast is 'celebrated July 24 in the Latin Church but on the third Sunday of July in the Maronite Church'. Brooklyn eparchy parish bulletin: 'Monday, February 9, 2026, Feast of Saint Maron, is a Holy Day of Obligation' (moved from 5 January in the 17th century). Both repo dates confirmed. The other fixed feasts (Presentation, Annunciation, Peter and Paul, Transfiguration, Assumption, Immaculate Conception) are universal dates not checked against a Maronite calendar; the list is a short selection. |
| Methodist Church in Britain | ◐ partial | Covenant Sunday, Aldersgate Sunday, Wesley Day, Watchnight, Remembrance Sunday | Covenant Sunday: "for many Methodist congregations, the first Sunday of the new year is marked as Covenant Sunday". Wesley Day: 24 May. Aldersgate Sunday: "Traditionally held on the Sunday preceding Wesley Day, 24 May", but the same page says that in 2026 the two fall on the same day, while the lectionary puts "Conversion of John and Charles Wesley" on 17 May 2026. Watchnight: the lectionary gives readings but no date; 31 December comes from memory (unverified). The red color of Wesley Day and Aldersgate Sunday has no source. Remembrance Sunday: "Held on the 2nd Sunday in November" (the lectionary: "or for REMEMBRANCE SUNDAY", 8 November 2026). The page's other special and themed Sundays (Racial Justice, Bible, Education Sunday and the like) are left out. |
| Moravian Church in America | ◐ partial | Moravian festival days | Colors only where the 2026 lectionary gives them; several festivals have none. |
| Ethiopian and Eritrean Orthodox Tewahedo | ◐ partial | Saints' days by Ethiopian date (9 rows): Michael 12 Hidar and 12 Sene, Gabriel 19 Tahsas and 19 Hamle, Aster'eyo Maryam 21 Tir, Kidane Mehret 16 Yekatit, St George 23 Miyazya, Lideta Maryam 1 Ginbot, Tekle Haymanot 24 Nehase | Dates confirmed: Michael Hedar 12 and Sene 12; Gabriel Tahsas 19 (Hamle given without a day number); Tir 21 = departure of St Mary; Kidane Mehret Yekatit 16; St George 23 Miyazya (1 May); Tekle Haymanot died the 24th of Nehase. Lideta 1 Ginbot only indirectly (church site lists Mary's Nativity as 'May 1'). Name of 21 Tir is wrong in the repo: 'Astemehro' should be 'Aster'eyo Maryam' (also spelled Astereyo/Asterio Mariam). Hamle 19 for Gabriel not confirmed from a source. |
| Syriac Orthodox Church of Antioch, Malankara Orthodox Syrian Church | ◐ partial | Fixed feasts: Presentation 2 Feb, Annunciation 25 Mar, Transfiguration 6 Aug, Shunoyo 15 Aug, Nativity of Mary 8 Sep, Sleebo 14 Sep, Nativity 25 Dec, Glorification of the Virgin 26 Dec, Circumcision 1 Jan, Denho 6 Jan, Peter and Paul 29 Jun | Confirmed: Presentation 2 Feb, Annunciation 25 Mar, Peter and Paul 29 Jun, St George 23 Apr (NEA), and all the fixed feasts kept in days.csv. Not confirmed: First church of the Virgin 15 Jun, Baselios Yeldo 2 Oct, Sergius and Bacchus 7 Oct, and St Thomas 3 Jul (NEA lists Thomas only on 21 Dec). NEA puts Jacob Baradaeus on 28 Nov and the Twelve Apostles on 30 Jun, while feasts.csv has "All Saints; Mor Yaqub Burdono" on 30 Jun. The sources also list Holy Innocents 27 Dec, Mary of the Sowing 15 Jan and Mary of the Harvest 15 May, which the repo leaves out. |
| Reformed (RCA / CRC) | ◐ partial | All Saints Day, Reformation Sunday and Reformation Day | The RCA's key-dates list has All Saints Day (1 November) as its only day that is not a day of the Lord. Its holidays and observances list adds Reformation Sunday, "Sunday before October 31 (unless October 31 falls on a Sunday, then it is just called Reformation Day)", and Reformation Day (31 October), which are in the calendar; its civic days and other observances (World Communion Sunday and the like) are left out. No CRCNA or Reformed Worship calendar was found online; for the CRC this is unverified. |
| Roman Catholic (General Roman Calendar) | ◐ partial | National (81), institutional (37) and personal-jurisdiction subcalendars | Left out: commemorations of the departed, dedications "if the date is unknown", observances of one branch of an order |

Gold when no other color is given. Light blue or white for the Theotokos. Purple or dark red for the Cross (red or green in some places for the Exaltation). Red for martyrs and for the Nativity and Apostles' Fasts. Green for Palm Sunday, Pentecost and the Day of the Holy Spirit. Black for Holy Week. Dark red on Great Thursday. White from the prokeimenon of the Holy Saturday Liturgy. White for Theophany and the Transfiguration. Blue for the Dormition fast and feast (Carpatho-Russian). Pascha: white until the end of Paschal Matins, then bright red through the Paschal season in Russian/Moscow use, but 'In Carpatho-Russian style, in the Paschal season, white, exclusively, is worn'. The repo differs in places: Great Lent is dark color throughout, but the source has 'the weekdays ... black' with dark colors only on Sundays and feasts. The Nativity season is white, but the source has 'Gold is worn from Christmas to Epiphany' (white on Christmas day in some places). See the discrepancies file. The source is a translation hosted by a convent website, not an official OCA page. |
| BCP 1979 (Episcopal Church) | ◐ partial | Season colors: purple/violet Advent (royal blue an option) and Lent (unbleached linen an option); red Holy Week and Pentecost; white Maundy Thursday; no color (altar stripped) Good Friday and Holy Saturday | The Episcopal Church's own dictionary says 'PURPLE/VIOLET for Advent (or ROYAL BLUE) & Lent (or UNBLEACHED LINEN)' and 'RED is used in Holy Week, the Day of Pentecost, and at ordinations.' It does not mention Maundy Thursday, Good Friday or Holy Saturday, so those remain unsourced. Purple is drawn for Advent and Lent, as listed first; royal blue and unbleached linen are named in the page readout. |
| LCMS Three-year (LSB), LCMS One-year (LSB), LCC Three-year (Lutheran Church–Canada), LCC One-year (Lutheran Church–Canada) | ◐ partial | Season and day colors (blue Advent, scarlet Holy Week, green after Pentecost/Trinity) | The LCMS calendar confirms blue (or violet) Advent; white for Christmas Eve/Day, Epiphany, Transfiguration (both series), All Saints, Trinity and Easter (gold allowed); violet or black for Ash Wednesday; scarlet from Palm Sunday through Maundy Thursday (violet allowed); black Good Friday; red Pentecost and Reformation. The Escue article (an essay from the LW era, not LSB itself) gives green Sundays after Epiphany and after Pentecost, a white Baptism, white minor festivals, and red for martyr festivals and Holy Cross. Disagreements: the LCMS calendar gives Ash Wednesday as "violet or black" (repo has BLACK) and Maundy Thursday as scarlet, "white may be used in some places" (repo has WHITE); the Altar Guild Manual prefers white when Communion is celebrated. No source opened confirms Holy Saturday black over white, Thanksgiving white, or the color of the Last Sunday (Escue suggests gold). LSB itself and the LSB Altar Book could not be read. Applied: Ash Wednesday stays black, which LSB lists first (published source over the website), with violet named in the readout; violet is named as the option for Advent and Holy Week, gold for Easter Day. Still open: Maundy Thursday, where LSB's first color was not confirmed (repo white). |
| Svenska kyrkan (Church of Sweden), Den norske kirke (Church of Norway), Folkekirken (Evangelical Lutheran Church in Denmark), Suomen ev.-lut. kirkko (Evangelical Lutheran Church of Finland) | ◐ partial | Season and feast colors | Norway: all of the repo's colors match the 2010 rule (Good Friday violet; the rule allows black as an alternative). Finland: matches the day pages except Maundy Thursday (source violet or blue, repo white) and All Saints (source red, or white at evening services; repo white). Denmark: the church prescribes no colors; the repo follows common custom, but Septuagesima and Sexagesima are purple in the repo where no source was found. Sweden: no national Kyrkohandbok color table was found online; parish pages confirm the seasons, St Stephen red, Maundy Thursday white, Apostles' Day red, St Michael and All Saints white. Trinity Sunday, the Sunday after All Saints, and Domssöndagen vary between sources. See 6-discrepancies.md. |
| Svenska kyrkan (Church of Sweden), Den norske kirke (Church of Norway), Folkekirken (Evangelical Lutheran Church in Denmark), Suomen ev.-lut. kirkko (Evangelical Lutheran Church of Finland) | ◐ partial | Season and feast colors | Docs say colors are each church's but name no source |
| Coptic Orthodox Church | ◐ partial | White vestments all year (the tonia is always white); in Holy Week the church is hung with black and icons are veiled, replaced by white for the Resurrection | The church prescribes no cycle of liturgical colors. What the sources describe is practice: the priest's tonia is 'a white tunic worn by all monks and priests while celebrating the Holy Mass', and in Holy Week 'black veils are draped over the walls and along the columns of the church' and icons are covered in black, then replaced with white for the Resurrection. So the black comes from the church's hangings, not the vestments; the repo's wording ('following the vestments', 'vestments ... black in Holy Week') should change. No source found for Covenant Thursday being white. |

Some Ruthenian titles differ: 'Beginning of the Church Year', 'Thomas Sunday', 'Entrance of the Mother of God into the Temple', 'St. Phillip's Fast', 'Sunday before the Nativity (Sunday of the Ancestors)'. SOR uses ma`alto, soboro, shunoyo, qoodosh `idto, denho and qotne da-gleeylo. The Malankara NEA diocese uses Ma`altho, Suboro, Shunoyo, Koodhosh Eetho, Denho, Yeldo, Slibo and Qyomto. "Suboro" is exactly the NEA spelling. No fetched source spells it "Mayaltho" (NEA: Ma`altho), and "Kothne" was seen only as SOR's "qotne". Neither is an error. |

## Errors found in sources

Where a source misprints a reading or a date, the calendar uses the corrected text. Each row names the source, what it has, what the calendar uses and why. **Edit `docs/source-errors.csv`.** A published book is preferred to a website unless the church has changed its use since the book was printed.

| Church | Source | What | Source has | Calendar uses | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BCP 1962 (Anglican Church of Canada) | Prayer Book Society of Canada, Lectionary Chart (Traditional) | All Saints Epistle | Revelation 7:9–19 | Revelation 7:9–17 | The chapter has 17 verses; the Society's own text of the propers |
| BCP 1962 (Anglican Church of Canada) | Book of Common Prayer (1962) PDF text, psalm table p. lii | St Michael and All Angels Introit | Psalm 103. 119–22 | Psalm 103:19–22 | Psalm 103 has 22 verses; the Society's chart has 103:19–22 |
| BCP 1962 (Anglican Church of Canada) | Book of Common Prayer (1962), anglican.ca PDF, p. 123 | Epiphany 1 Epistle heading | Romans 12. 11. | Romans 12:1–5 | The text begins "I beseech you therefore, brethren, by the mercies of God" (Romans 12:1) |
| BCP 1962 (Anglican Church of Canada) | Book of Common Prayer (1962), anglican.ca PDF, p. 109 | St Stephen Lesson heading | Acts 55. | Acts 7:55–60 | The text is Acts 7:55–60 ("Stephen, being full of the Holy Spirit ... he fell asleep") |

<a id="anglican-common-worship"></a>
### Common Worship (Church of England)

`anglican-common-worship` · Anglican

| Aspect | Status | What | Source | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| days | ✓ sourced | Epiphany season to the Presentation (2 Feb), Sundays before Lent, Sundays after Trinity, Sundays before Advent from All Saints to Christ the King | Church of England, Common Worship, The Church's Year: Rules, [churchofengland.org/…](https://www.churchofengland.org/prayer-and-worship/worship-texts-and-resources/common-worship/churchs-year/rules) | Saved October 2026. The Rules' precedence and transfers are applied (festivals off the Sundays of Advent, Lent and Eastertide and off Principal Feasts and Holy Days; Holy Week and Easter Week to the Monday after Easter 2; lesser festivals on Sundays and festivals omitted); the optional Sundays (Epiphany 2-8 January, Candlemas 28 January-3 February, All Saints 30 October-5 November) are shown beside the date, marked (observed) or All Saints' Sunday. |
| feasts | ✓ sourced | Fixed holy days and commemorations: dates and names | anglicanlectionary.com | Taken from each book's calendar on the site; book lists kept in tools/anglican_books/ |
| feasts | ✓ sourced | Principal Feasts, Festivals, Lesser Festivals (roman) and Commemorations (italic) | Church of England, Common Worship, The Church's Year: The Calendar, [churchofengland.org/…](https://www.churchofengland.org/prayer-and-worship/worship-texts-and-resources/common-worship/churchs-year/calendar) | Saved October 2026. Accession of Charles III from 2021-22, of Elizabeth II before. The Dedication Festival has no fixed date. |
| colors | ≈ inferred | Feast colors: red for apostles, evangelists (except St John), martyrs, Holy Cross Day and the Holy Innocents; white for feasts of Our Lord, the BVM, St Joseph, the Birth of John the Baptist, Mary Magdalene, Michael and All Angels and St John; lesser festivals red (martyrs), white or the season | Church in Wales, Lectionary Year A, Advent 2025 - Kingdom 2026 (English), 'Liturgical Colours' note (scheme agreed by the Bench of Bishops, June 2011), [churchinwales.contentfiles.net/…](https://churchinwales.contentfiles.net/media/documents/2455_-_Lectionary_Year_A_WD_2a_-_Advent_2025_-_Kingdom_2026_-_English.pdf); Wikipedia, 'Liturgical colours' (last resort; Anglican section summarizes the Church of England's published Lectionary), [en.wikipedia.org/…](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liturgical_colours) | The Church in Wales note states the rule outright for its book (sourced for wales-1984): 'Apostles and Evangelists (except John the Evangelist), Holy Cross Day, Holy Innocents in RED'; BVM, Joseph, Birth of John the Baptist, Mary Magdalene, Transfiguration, Michael and All Angels, John the Evangelist in WHITE; lesser festivals 'RED (for Martyrs) or WHITE or the colour of the season'. Wikipedia says the Church of England lectionary follows post-1969 Roman usage, which has the same pattern. For the other books (which print no colors) the rule is applied by analogy. The repo already does the St John and Holy Cross exceptions, but the detail text should say so. See discrepancies file for bcp-1962 Conversion of St Paul and wales-1984 Martyrdom of Paul. No Anglican Church of Canada source on the Conversion of St Paul's color was found. GIRM 346(b) (usccb.org) gives red for apostles, evangelists and martyrs in Roman use, which the Anglican schemes follow. Applied: bcp-1962 Conversion of St Paul white; wales-1984 Conversion and Martyrdom of St Paul red from 2011. |
| colors | ✓ sourced | Season colors with the options named (blue in Advent, Lent array, red in the Kingdom season, rose on Advent 3 and Lent 4); Good Friday red for the liturgy, hangings removed; no color on Easter Eve | Church of England, Common Worship, The Church's Year: Rules, [churchofengland.org/…](https://www.churchofengland.org/prayer-and-worship/worship-texts-and-resources/common-worship/churchs-year/rules); Space and Colour page; Times and Seasons (2006) | "Coloured hangings are traditionally removed for Good Friday and Easter Eve, but red is the colour for the liturgy on Good Friday." |
| readings | ✓ sourced | Principal, Second and Third Service; Daily Eucharistic Lectionary | Church of England, Common Worship, The Church's Year: Lectionary, [churchofengland.org/…](https://www.churchofengland.org/prayer-and-worship/worship-texts-and-resources/common-worship/churchs-year/lectionary); Common Worship: Services and Prayers (2000) Lectionary as corrected (Lectionary.pdf); Weekday Lectionary (2005, 2010 additions) | Saved October 2026. Two readings follow the 2005 corrections where the website lags (see source-errors.csv). |
| art | ~ guess | Center art: Canterbury cross roundel |  | Drawn for this project (tools/make_emblems.py), CC0; chosen as the church's picture |
| other | ~ guess | Bible version for reading links (KJV; NRSVA; ESV; RSVCE) |  | Set in tradition.csv; a choice, no source |

<a id="anglican-bcp-1662"></a>
### BCP 1662 (Church of England)

`anglican-bcp-1662` · Anglican

| Aspect | Status | What | Source | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| days | ≈ inferred | Historic year: Sundays after Epiphany, Septuagesima/Sexagesima/Quinquagesima, Sundays after Easter and Trinity, Sunday next before Advent |  | Built from the books' known structure; no source cited for the rules (data/Anglican/days.csv) |
| feasts | ✓ sourced | Fixed holy days and commemorations: dates and names | anglicanlectionary.com | Taken from each book's calendar on the site; book lists kept in tools/anglican_books/ |
| colors | ~ guess | Season colors: purple Advent, Pre-Lent and Lent, red Holy Week and Whitsuntide, white festivals, green otherwise | Wikipedia, 'Liturgical colours' (last resort; Anglican section summarizes the Church of England's published Lectionary), [en.wikipedia.org/…](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liturgical_colours); Church in Wales, Lectionary Year A, Advent 2025 - Kingdom 2026 (English), 'Liturgical Colours' note (scheme agreed by the Bench of Bishops, June 2011), [churchinwales.contentfiles.net/…](https://churchinwales.contentfiles.net/media/documents/2455_-_Lectionary_Year_A_WD_2a_-_Advent_2025_-_Kingdom_2026_-_English.pdf) | No prayer book prescribes colors, and no source was found for a 'customary' scheme for the historic books. Wikipedia says most Anglican churches use the Roman colors, usually the post-1969 form, with some using the earlier form (black on Good Friday) - which is roughly what the repo does (pre-1969 Roman: violet Septuagesima, red Pentecost octave). For wales-1984 the Church in Wales 2011 scheme directly contradicts the repo (white to Candlemas, green 3 Feb to Shrove Tuesday, green from the Monday after Pentecost): wales-1984 should be split out and sourced from that note. No Anglican Church of Canada guidance on colors (BCP 1962 or BAS) was found online. wales-1984 now follows the 2011 scheme from 2011 on; this row covers it only before then. |

Wikipedia says the Church of England lectionary follows post-1969 Roman usage, which has the same pattern. For the other books (which print no colors) the rule is applied by analogy. The repo already does the St John and Holy Cross exceptions, but the detail text should say so. See discrepancies file for bcp-1962 Conversion of St Paul and wales-1984 Martyrdom of Paul. No Anglican Church of Canada source on the Conversion of St Paul's color was found. GIRM 346(b) (usccb.org) gives red for apostles, evangelists and martyrs in Roman use, which the Anglican schemes follow. Applied: bcp-1962 Conversion of St Paul white; wales-1984 Conversion and Martyrdom of St Paul red from 2011. |

It does not mention Maundy Thursday, Good Friday or Holy Saturday, so those remain unsourced. Purple is drawn for Advent and Lent, as listed first; royal blue and unbleached linen are named in the page readout. |

| colors | ~ guess | Season colors: purple Advent, Pre-Lent and Lent, red Holy Week and Whitsuntide, white festivals, green otherwise | Wikipedia, 'Liturgical colours' (last resort; Anglican section summarizes the Church of England's published Lectionary), [en.wikipedia.org/…](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liturgical_colours); Church in Wales, Lectionary Year A, Advent 2025 - Kingdom 2026 (English), 'Liturgical Colours' note (scheme agreed by the Bench of Bishops, June 2011), [churchinwales.contentfiles.net/…](https://churchinwales.contentfiles.net/media/documents/2455_-_Lectionary_Year_A_WD_2a_-_Advent_2025_-_Kingdom_2026_-_English.pdf) | No prayer book prescribes colors, and no source was found for a 'customary' scheme for the historic books. Wikipedia says most Anglican churches use the Roman colors, usually the post-1969 form, with some using the earlier form (black on Good Friday) - which is roughly what the repo does (pre-1969 Roman: violet Septuagesima, red Pentecost octave). For wales-1984 the Church in Wales 2011 scheme directly contradicts the repo (white to Candlemas, green 3 Feb to Shrove Tuesday, green from the Monday after Pentecost): wales-1984 should be split out and sourced from that note. No Anglican Church of Canada guidance on colors (BCP 1962 or BAS) was found online. wales-1984 now follows the 2011 scheme from 2011 on; this row covers it only before then. |
| colors | ≈ inferred | Feast colors: red for apostles, evangelists (except St John), martyrs, Holy Cross Day and the Holy Innocents; white for feasts of Our Lord, the BVM, St Joseph, the Birth of John the Baptist, Mary Magdalene, Michael and All Angels and St John; lesser festivals red (martyrs), white or the season | Church in Wales, Lectionary Year A, Advent 2025 - Kingdom 2026 (English), 'Liturgical Colours' note (scheme agreed by the Bench of Bishops, June 2011), [churchinwales.contentfiles.net/…](https://churchinwales.contentfiles.net/media/documents/2455_-_Lectionary_Year_A_WD_2a_-_Advent_2025_-_Kingdom_2026_-_English.pdf); Wikipedia, 'Liturgical colours' (last resort; Anglican section summarizes the Church of England's published Lectionary), [en.wikipedia.org/…](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liturgical_colours) | The Church in Wales note states the rule outright for its book (sourced for wales-1984): 'Apostles and Evangelists (except John the Evangelist), Holy Cross Day, Holy Innocents in RED'; BVM, Joseph, Birth of John the Baptist, Mary Magdalene, Transfiguration, Michael and All Angels, John the Evangelist in WHITE; lesser festivals 'RED (for Martyrs) or WHITE or the colour of the season'. Wikipedia says the Church of England lectionary follows post-1969 Roman usage, which has the same pattern. For the other books (which print no colors) the rule is applied by analogy. The repo already does the St John and Holy Cross exceptions, but the detail text should say so. See discrepancies file for bcp-1962 Conversion of St Paul and wales-1984 Martyrdom of Paul. No Anglican Church of Canada source on the Conversion of St Paul's color was found. GIRM 346(b) (usccb.org) gives red for apostles, evangelists and martyrs in Roman use, which the Anglican schemes follow. Applied: bcp-1962 Conversion of St Paul white; wales-1984 Conversion and Martyrdom of St Paul red from 2011. |
| readings | ≈ inferred | 25th/26th Sundays after Trinity take the 5th/6th after Epiphany | Book of Common Prayer (1928) rubric | Worked out from the rubric |

The book gives the first verse only; the last is that of the printed text. Undated propers (a Saint's Day, Dedication, Marriage, Burial) not entered. |
| readings | ✓ sourced | Table of Psalms and Lessons for the Christian Year (1943 lectionary, as printed 1945): every day's psalms and lessons at Morning and Evening Prayer, the Sundays' alternative sets, the fixed holy days' Eve, Morning and Evening, Independence Day, Thanksgiving Day and the End of the Year | The Lectionary (1945), [justus.anglican.org/…](https://justus.anglican.org/resources/bcp/1928/Lectionary_1945.pdf); checked against [episcopalnet.org/…](https://www.episcopalnet.org/1928bcp/readings/index.html) | Read October 2026 from the PDF's text layer (pp. 3-20), doubtful lines checked against the page images and episcopalnet's day-by-day lessons. Open-ended references kept as '-end'. Starred Sunday lessons not marked; special occasions without a date (dedication, patronal festivals, ordinations ...) not entered. |

<a id="anglican-wales-1984"></a>
### BCP 1984 (Church in Wales)

`anglican-wales-1984` · Anglican

| Aspect | Status | What | Source | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| days | ≈ inferred | Sundays and movable days follow each book's structure (historic year for Wales, Divine Worship, Proposed 2025, IARB; RCL-style year for the Portuguese modern books) | anglicanlectionary.com | docs: the site's Sunday names and colors are generic, so the Sundays follow the book's structure instead |
| days | ✓ sourced | From Advent 2003 (year ge 2003): Sundays of Advent and of Christmas; the Epiphany of Our Lord; the Baptism of Christ (the First, or Second, Sunday of Epiphany) and Sundays of Epiphany to 1 February; Fifth to Second Sunday before Lent (the Second, Creation Sunday, only after 2 February) and the Sunday before Lent (Transfiguration Sunday); Sundays of Lent (Mothering Sunday, Passion Sunday, Palm Sunday the Sixth); Sundays of Easter (Rogation Sunday, Sunday after Ascension Day); the Day of Pentecost; Thanksgiving for Holy Communion (Thursday after Trinity Sunday); Sundays after Trinity to the Last Sunday after Trinity (Bible Sunday, five Sundays before Advent); First to Third Sunday of the Kingdom and Christ the King - the Fourth Sunday of the Kingdom | Church in Wales, The Book of Common Prayer for use in the Church in Wales: The New Calendar and the Collects (April 2003), [churchinwales.org.uk/…](https://www.churchinwales.org.uk/documents/364/New_Calendar__Collects.pdf); its revision, Revised New Calendar and Collects (2022), [churchinwales.org.uk/…](https://www.churchinwales.org.uk/documents/3535/2318_-_Revised_New_Calendar_and_Collects_-_Bilingual.pdf); Lectionary Year A, Advent 2025 - Kingdom 2026 (English), [churchinwales.contentfiles.net/…](https://churchinwales.contentfiles.net/media/documents/2455_-_Lectionary_Year_A_WD_2a_-_Advent_2025_-_Kingdom_2026_-_English.pdf); Wikipedia, 'Kingdomtide' (Kingdom season 'formally enshrined in the revised Church in Wales Lectionary of 2004'), [en.wikipedia.org/…](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdomtide) | Read October 2026; the 2003 and 2022 books only through WebFetch extracts. Start year 2003 is inferred: the book is dated April 2003, and no statement of the date it came into use was found (Advent 2003 assumed; Wikipedia's 'Lectionary of 2004' could mean Advent 2004). The 2003 book numbers the Sundays after Pentecost (Trinity Sunday the First, with Trinity 1-21 beside them); the 2022 book and the booklets say 'after Trinity', which is used for every year from 2003. The 1984 Sundays hold before 2003. Ring labels from 2003: Epiphany (to 2 February), Ordinary Time, Lent, Holy Week, Easter (to Pentecost), Ordinary Time, Kingdom (from 1 November). Colors from 2003 to 2010 are still the generic prayer-book ones (no source for that period). |
| feasts | ✓ sourced | Fixed holy days and commemorations: dates and names | anglicanlectionary.com | Taken from each book's calendar on the site; book lists kept in tools/anglican_books/ |
| feasts | ✓ sourced | From Advent 2003: the holy days, lesser festivals and commemorations of the New Calendar (Thomas 3 July, the Visit of the Virgin Mary to Elizabeth 31 May, Matthias 14 May, Hilary 13 January, Kentigern 14 January, Timothy and Titus 26 January, Cadoc 25 September, Peblig 4 July, Gregory the Great 3 September, Monica 27 August, Leo 10 November and others moved; Woolos, Griffith Jones, Ann Griffiths, William Morgan, Daniel Rowland and other newer commemorations added; James of Jerusalem no longer a red-letter day), with names as the 2025-26 booklet prints them | Church in Wales, Lectionary Year A, Advent 2025 - Kingdom 2026 (English): the day headings, '*' (groups I-II) and '†' (IV-V) marks, [churchinwales.contentfiles.net/…](https://churchinwales.contentfiles.net/media/documents/2455_-_Lectionary_Year_A_WD_2a_-_Advent_2025_-_Kingdom_2026_-_English.pdf); Wikipedia, 'Calendar of saints (Church in Wales)', for days that fall on Sundays or in Holy Week and Easter Week in 2025-26, [en.wikipedia.org/…](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calendar_of_saints_%28Church_in_Wales%29); The New Calendar and the Collects (2003) for Thomas, the Visit, Hilary, Cadoc, Peblig | The list is the current (2022/2026 revision) calendar, applied from 2003; changes between 2003 and 2022 are not modeled. About 35 days were not visible in the 2025-26 booklet (on a Sunday or displaced) and come from Wikipedia, whose list mixes in some superseded dates (those were dropped where the booklet shows the other date); see wales-cal/notes.md. Full names for new rows are built from the Wikipedia entries. |
| colors | ~ guess | Season colors: purple Advent, Pre-Lent and Lent, red Holy Week and Whitsuntide, white festivals, green otherwise | Wikipedia, 'Liturgical colours' (last resort; Anglican section summarizes the Church of England's published Lectionary), [en.wikipedia.org/…](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liturgical_colours); Church in Wales, Lectionary Year A, Advent 2025 - Kingdom 2026 (English), 'Liturgical Colours' note (scheme agreed by the Bench of Bishops, June 2011), [churchinwales.contentfiles.net/…](https://churchinwales.contentfiles.net/media/documents/2455_-_Lectionary_Year_A_WD_2a_-_Advent_2025_-_Kingdom_2026_-_English.pdf) | No prayer book prescribes colors, and no source was found for a 'customary' scheme for the historic books. Wikipedia says most Anglican churches use the Roman colors, usually the post-1969 form, with some using the earlier form (black on Good Friday) - which is roughly what the repo does (pre-1969 Roman: violet Septuagesima, red Pentecost octave). For wales-1984 the Church in Wales 2011 scheme directly contradicts the repo (white to Candlemas, green 3 Feb to Shrove Tuesday, green from the Monday after Pentecost): wales-1984 should be split out and sourced from that note. No Anglican Church of Canada guidance on colors (BCP 1962 or BAS) was found online. wales-1984 now follows the 2011 scheme from 2011 on; this row covers it only before then. |
| colors | ≈ inferred | Feast colors: red for apostles, evangelists (except St John), martyrs, Holy Cross Day and the Holy Innocents; white for feasts of Our Lord, the BVM, St Joseph, the Birth of John the Baptist, Mary Magdalene, Michael and All Angels and St John; lesser festivals red (martyrs), white or the season | Church in Wales, Lectionary Year A, Advent 2025 - Kingdom 2026 (English), 'Liturgical Colours' note (scheme agreed by the Bench of Bishops, June 2011), [churchinwales.contentfiles.net/…](https://churchinwales.contentfiles.net/media/documents/2455_-_Lectionary_Year_A_WD_2a_-_Advent_2025_-_Kingdom_2026_-_English.pdf); Wikipedia, 'Liturgical colours' (last resort; Anglican section summarizes the Church of England's published Lectionary), [en.wikipedia.org/…](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liturgical_colours) | The Church in Wales note states the rule outright for its book (sourced for wales-1984): 'Apostles and Evangelists (except John the Evangelist), Holy Cross Day, Holy Innocents in RED'; BVM, Joseph, Birth of John the Baptist, Mary Magdalene, Transfiguration, Michael and All Angels, John the Evangelist in WHITE; lesser festivals 'RED (for Martyrs) or WHITE or the colour of the season'. Wikipedia says the Church of England lectionary follows post-1969 Roman usage, which has the same pattern. For the other books (which print no colors) the rule is applied by analogy. The repo already does the St John and Holy Cross exceptions, but the detail text should say so. See discrepancies file for bcp-1962 Conversion of St Paul and wales-1984 Martyrdom of Paul. No Anglican Church of Canada source on the Conversion of St Paul's color was found. GIRM 346(b) (usccb.org) gives red for apostles, evangelists and martyrs in Roman use, which the Anglican schemes follow. Applied: bcp-1962 Conversion of St Paul white; wales-1984 Conversion and Martyrdom of St Paul red from 2011. |
| colors | ✓ sourced | Church in Wales colors: purple Advent; white Christmas to Candlemas (2 Feb); green 3 Feb to Shrove Tuesday; purple Lent (Passion red optional on Passion Sunday); Passion red or purple Palm Sunday and Holy Week, white at the Maundy Thursday Eucharist; white Easter Day to the eve of Pentecost; red Pentecost; green from the Monday after Pentecost (white Trinity Sunday; red optional in the Kingdom season); white Christ the King; All Souls purple (or black) | Church in Wales, Lectionary Year A, Advent 2025 - Kingdom 2026 (English), 'Liturgical Colours' note (scheme agreed by the Bench of Bishops, June 2011), [churchinwales.contentfiles.net/…](https://churchinwales.contentfiles.net/media/documents/2455_-_Lectionary_Year_A_WD_2a_-_Advent_2025_-_Kingdom_2026_-_English.pdf) | Applied from the 2011-12 church year on (year ge 2011); the 1984 colors of the generic prayer-book row hold for the years before. Black is named for Good Friday, purple for Easter Eve, red for the Kingdom season. |
| names | ✓ sourced | Feast names in each book's language (Portuguese for the Brazil/Portugal books), as the site gives them | anglicanlectionary.com | Site's feast names kept |

When the Nativity falls on a Sunday (2022), the OCA has no Sunday after Nativity: 26 Dec 2022 takes its readings (Gal 1:11-19, Matt 2:13-23) under the Synaxis. Jan 1 2023 is 'Sunday before Theophany'. The UGCC calls this the 'Gregorian style'; it gives the same dates as the Revised Julian until 2800. The decision is for the UGCC in Ukraine; diaspora eparchies decide for themselves. Eastern Orthodox part (which churches use which calendar) not checked against a fetched source. |

On 12 Nov 2023, after the calendar change, he addressed pilgrims 'on the day of his [Josaphat's] memory'. Parish sites: Holy Eucharist is kept on the 'Thursday after the Sunday of All Saints' (Thursday 19 June 2025 = Pascha+60), often transferred to the following Sunday; a forum post says the UGCC Particular Law lists it. St Josaphat: 'The actual feast ... takes place in 12 November' (UGCC eparchy, new calendar); before 2023 the UGCC kept 12 Nov on the Julian calendar (25 Nov civil). Ruthenian: the Archeparchy of Pittsburgh's 2026 calendar has no entry for either (nothing on Thursday 4 June or on 12 Nov 2026), so the repo's ruthenian variant is unconfirmed. Parish and forum sources only; no official UGCC calendar page was fetched. |
| colors | ◐ partial | Slavic color cycle after the Russian Nastolnaya Kniga with Carpatho-Russian (Ruthenian) local customs: gold default, blue Theotokos, dark red/purple Cross, black Lenten weekdays and Holy Week, red fasts and martyrs, green Palm Sunday/Pentecost, white Pascha | "The Liturgical Colors of Vestments in the Orthodox Church", The Catalog of Good Deeds (St Elisabeth Convent, Minsk), 28 Dec 2016, translating Nastol'naya Kniga Sviashchenno-sluzhitelia vol. 4 (Moscow, 1983) via The Messenger of St Andrew's Russian Orthodox Cathedral (Philadelphia, 1999), [catalog.obitel-minsk.com/…](https://catalog.obitel-minsk.com/blog/2016/12/the-liturgical-colors-of-vestments-in); OrthodoxWiki, Liturgical colors, [orthodoxwiki.org/…](https://orthodoxwiki.org/Liturgical_colors) | Confirms the following. Gold when no other color is given. Light blue or white for the Theotokos. Purple or dark red for the Cross (red or green in some places for the Exaltation). Red for martyrs and for the Nativity and Apostles' Fasts. Green for Palm Sunday, Pentecost and the Day of the Holy Spirit. Black for Holy Week. Dark red on Great Thursday. White from the prokeimenon of the Holy Saturday Liturgy. White for Theophany and the Transfiguration. Blue for the Dormition fast and feast (Carpatho-Russian). Pascha: white until the end of Paschal Matins, then bright red through the Paschal season in Russian/Moscow use, but 'In Carpatho-Russian style, in the Paschal season, white, exclusively, is worn'. The repo differs in places: Great Lent is dark color throughout, but the source has 'the weekdays ... black' with dark colors only on Sundays and feasts. The Nativity season is white, but the source has 'Gold is worn from Christmas to Epiphany' (white on Christmas day in some places). See the discrepancies file. The source is a translation hosted by a convent website, not an official OCA page. |

On 12 Nov 2023, after the calendar change, he addressed pilgrims 'on the day of his [Josaphat's] memory'. Parish sites: Holy Eucharist is kept on the 'Thursday after the Sunday of All Saints' (Thursday 19 June 2025 = Pascha+60), often transferred to the following Sunday; a forum post says the UGCC Particular Law lists it. St Josaphat: 'The actual feast ... takes place in 12 November' (UGCC eparchy, new calendar); before 2023 the UGCC kept 12 Nov on the Julian calendar (25 Nov civil). Ruthenian: the Archeparchy of Pittsburgh's 2026 calendar has no entry for either (nothing on Thursday 4 June or on 12 Nov 2026), so the repo's ruthenian variant is unconfirmed. Parish and forum sources only; no official UGCC calendar page was fetched. |

Gold when no other color is given. Light blue or white for the Theotokos. Purple or dark red for the Cross (red or green in some places for the Exaltation). Red for martyrs and for the Nativity and Apostles' Fasts. Green for Palm Sunday, Pentecost and the Day of the Holy Spirit. Black for Holy Week. Dark red on Great Thursday. White from the prokeimenon of the Holy Saturday Liturgy. White for Theophany and the Transfiguration. Blue for the Dormition fast and feast (Carpatho-Russian). Pascha: white until the end of Paschal Matins, then bright red through the Paschal season in Russian/Moscow use, but 'In Carpatho-Russian style, in the Paschal season, white, exclusively, is worn'. The repo differs in places: Great Lent is dark color throughout, but the source has 'the weekdays ... black' with dark colors only on Sundays and feasts. The Nativity season is white, but the source has 'Gold is worn from Christmas to Epiphany' (white on Christmas day in some places). See the discrepancies file. The source is a translation hosted by a convent website, not an official OCA page. |

Escue, "Colors of the Liturgical Seasons" (Lutheran Worship Notes 29, 1994), LCMS Worship, [lcms.org/…](https://www.lcms.org/worship/church-year/colors-of-liturgical-seasons); "How to Prepare the Church Sanctuary for Lent and Holy Week" (from The Altar Guild Manual: LSB Edition, pp. 80-83), CPH blog, [blog.cph.org/…](https://blog.cph.org/worship/preparing-the-church-sanctuary-for-lent-and-holy-week) | The LCMS calendar confirms blue (or violet) Advent; white for Christmas Eve/Day, Epiphany, Transfiguration (both series), All Saints, Trinity and Easter (gold allowed); violet or black for Ash Wednesday; scarlet from Palm Sunday through Maundy Thursday (violet allowed); black Good Friday; red Pentecost and Reformation. The Escue article (an essay from the LW era, not LSB itself) gives green Sundays after Epiphany and after Pentecost, a white Baptism, white minor festivals, and red for martyr festivals and Holy Cross. Disagreements: the LCMS calendar gives Ash Wednesday as "violet or black" (repo has BLACK) and Maundy Thursday as scarlet, "white may be used in some places" (repo has WHITE); the Altar Guild Manual prefers white when Communion is celebrated. No source opened confirms Holy Saturday black over white, Thanksgiving white, or the color of the Last Sunday (Escue suggests gold). LSB itself and the LSB Altar Book could not be read. Applied: Ash Wednesday stays black, which LSB lists first (published source over the website), with violet named in the readout; violet is named as the option for Advent and Holy Week, gold for Easter Day. Still open: Maundy Thursday, where LSB's first color was not confirmed (repo white). |

K. Escue, "Colors of the Liturgical Seasons" (Lutheran Worship Notes 29, 1994), LCMS Worship, [lcms.org/…](https://www.lcms.org/worship/church-year/colors-of-liturgical-seasons); "How to Prepare the Church Sanctuary for Lent and Holy Week" (from The Altar Guild Manual: LSB Edition, pp. 80-83), CPH blog, [blog.cph.org/…](https://blog.cph.org/worship/preparing-the-church-sanctuary-for-lent-and-holy-week) | The LCMS calendar confirms blue (or violet) Advent; white for Christmas Eve/Day, Epiphany, Transfiguration (both series), All Saints, Trinity and Easter (gold allowed); violet or black for Ash Wednesday; scarlet from Palm Sunday through Maundy Thursday (violet allowed); black Good Friday; red Pentecost and Reformation. The Escue article (an essay from the LW era, not LSB itself) gives green Sundays after Epiphany and after Pentecost, a white Baptism, white minor festivals, and red for martyr festivals and Holy Cross. Disagreements: the LCMS calendar gives Ash Wednesday as "violet or black" (repo has BLACK) and Maundy Thursday as scarlet, "white may be used in some places" (repo has WHITE); the Altar Guild Manual prefers white when Communion is celebrated. No source opened confirms Holy Saturday black over white, Thanksgiving white, or the color of the Last Sunday (Escue suggests gold). LSB itself and the LSB Altar Book could not be read. Applied: Ash Wednesday stays black, which LSB lists first (published source over the website), with violet named in the readout; violet is named as the option for Advent and Holy Week, gold for Easter Day. Still open: Maundy Thursday, where LSB's first color was not confirmed (repo white). |

<a id="lcms-tlh-one-year"></a>
### LCMS One-year (TLH)

`lcms-tlh-one-year` · Lutheran

| Aspect | Status | What | Source | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| days | ✓ sourced | Calendar of the one-year series | The Lutheran Hymnal (1941), The Calendar, p. [3] | One to six Sundays after Epiphany ending with Transfiguration; 22-27 Sundays after Trinity. |
| feasts | ✓ sourced | Red-letter (dated) days | The Lutheran Hymnal (1941), The Calendar, p. [3] |  |
| colors | ≈ inferred | All colors | Common Service Book of the Lutheran Church (1917), General Rubrics, "Paraments" (the Liturgical Colors), transcription at [acollectionofprayers.com/…](https://acollectionofprayers.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/01/nn_general-rubrics-common-service-book-of-the-lutheran-church.pdf) | The CSB rubric was read: violet from Advent to the Eve of the Nativity and through Lent to the Eve of Easter, except Good Friday; black on Good Friday; white from the Eve of the Nativity through the Epiphany season (St. Stephen red), from Easter to the Saturday before Whitsunday, and on the Transfiguration, Presentation, Annunciation, Visitation, St. Michael, and Trinity and its octave; red at Whitsuntide, on the Reformation and the Sunday after it, on apostles' and evangelists' days (St. John excepted), martyrs' days, All Saints, Harvest and Thanksgiving; green from Septuagesima to Ash Wednesday and from the second Sunday after Trinity. The repo matches, including the Trinity octave (easter+56 to +63). Still inferred, because TLH and the ELHB print no colors themselves. Gray areas: Holy Innocents and the Conversion of St. Paul fall in the white Epiphany period, where only St. Stephen is excepted, but they are also martyr and apostle days (red); the repo leaves both in the season color. The Lutheran Liturgy (1948) was not found online. |
| names | ✓ sourced | Latin names of Sundays in Lent and after Easter | The Lutheran Hymnal (1941) | On by default. |
| readings | ✓ sourced | Epistles and Gospels of propers and minor festivals | The Lutheran Hymnal (1941), Lutheran Library text edition, [lutheranlibrary.org/…](https://www.lutheranlibrary.org/424-the-lutheran-hymnal/) | Read October 2026. TLH has no OT lessons or psalms. Harvest, Mission Festival, Dedication, Day of Humiliation and Prayer left out (no date). |
| readings | ✓ sourced | Old Testament lessons and Second Series Epistles and Gospels; First Series and minor festivals checked | The Lutheran Hymnal (CPH, 1941) pp. 159-161, photographed by the maintainer, October 2026 | Added from the Table of Lessons. Matins and Vespers psalms, and the Lessons for Morning and Evening (pp. 161-164), are not yet entered. |
| readings | ✓ sourced | Lessons for Morning and Evening; Psalms for the Sundays, Feasts and Festivals; the Psalter over thirty-one days; the Matins and Vespers psalms of the Table of Lessons | The Lutheran Hymnal (CPH, 1941) pp. 159-166, photographed by the maintainer, October 2026 | Transcribed from the photographs and compared line by line with the ELHB's table, which it follows; differences kept as TLH prints them except the evident misprints listed under Errors found in sources. Tuesday after Quinquagesima (Luke 6:43–49) and Saturday after Trinity 21 (Ephesians 5:22–33) corrected after the maintainer checked them. |
| readings | ✓ sourced | Reading the Word of God: the three-year daily Bible reading plan of the LCC, LCMS and NALC (data/readings/plan-rwg.csv; the Bible reading plan box) | LCC, LCMS and NALC, Reading the Word of God, daily reading calendars for 2018, 2019 and 2020 (files.lcms.org), with its introduction; reporter.lcms.org, 10 June 2021, [reporter.lcms.org/…](https://reporter.lcms.org/2021/three-year-schedule-of-daily-bible-readings-for-2021-to-2023/) ; the 2021 calendar, [files.lcms.org/…](https://files.lcms.org/api/file/preview/9518A86C-4871-423A-9D37-57CED1D703F8) | Kept by the calendar year: the 2018, 2019 and 2020 cycles in turn (2021-23 republished them; the 2021 calendar has 2018's festival readings and sequence). The engine reproduces the three printed calendars day for day. Inferred, not printed: a leap year of the 2018 or 2019 cycle has no set on its last plan day, and a common year of the 2020 cycle reads its last two sets on 31 December. Four slips of the print corrected (see Errors found in sources). |

<a id="lcms-elhb-one-year"></a>
### LCMS One-year (ELHB)

`lcms-elhb-one-year` · Lutheran

| Aspect | Status | What | Source | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| days | ✓ sourced | Calendar and Introit/Collect headings | Evangelical Lutheran Hymn-Book (1912; 1909 printing, pp. 13-20 and 57-90), [archive.org/…](https://archive.org/details/evangelicalluthe09evan) | No Transfiguration Sunday (sixth Sunday after Epiphany has its collect); up to 27 Sundays after Trinity. |
| feasts | ✓ sourced | Festivals of p. 13 | Evangelical Lutheran Hymn-Book (1912), p. 13 | Reformation and apostles' days. |
| colors | ≈ inferred | All colors | Common Service Book of the Lutheran Church (1917), General Rubrics, "Paraments" (the Liturgical Colors), transcription at [acollectionofprayers.com/…](https://acollectionofprayers.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/01/nn_general-rubrics-common-service-book-of-the-lutheran-church.pdf) | The CSB rubric was read: violet from Advent to the Eve of the Nativity and through Lent to the Eve of Easter, except Good Friday; black on Good Friday; white from the Eve of the Nativity through the Epiphany season (St. Stephen red), from Easter to the Saturday before Whitsunday, and on the Transfiguration, Presentation, Annunciation, Visitation, St. Michael, and Trinity and its octave; red at Whitsuntide, on the Reformation and the Sunday after it, on apostles' and evangelists' days (St. John excepted), martyrs' days, All Saints, Harvest and Thanksgiving; green from Septuagesima to Ash Wednesday and from the second Sunday after Trinity. The repo matches, including the Trinity octave (easter+56 to +63). Still inferred, because TLH and the ELHB print no colors themselves. Gray areas: Holy Innocents and the Conversion of St. Paul fall in the white Epiphany period, where only St. Stephen is excepted, but they are also martyr and apostle days (red); the repo leaves both in the season color. The Lutheran Liturgy (1948) was not found online. |

| readings | ✓ sourced | Epistles/Gospels, OT and NT lessons, Morning and Evening table | Evangelical Lutheran Hymn-Book (1912; 1909 printing), [archive.org/…](https://archive.org/details/evangelicalluthe09evan) | Misprints corrected (checked against the print by the owner). Uncertain: Friday after Lent 5 evening, Isaiah 66:1-9. Last eight weeks counted back from Advent per rubric. |

Sweden: Svenska kyrkan parish pages: Ystad-Sövestads församling, "Lär dig mer om: Kyrkoårets fem färger", [svenskakyrkan.se/…](https://www.svenskakyrkan.se/ystad/lar-dig-mer-om-kyrkoarets-fem-farger); Sollentuna, "Kyrkoåret och kyrkans högtider", [svenskakyrkan.se/…](https://www.svenskakyrkan.se/sollentuna/kyrkoaret-och-kyrkans-hogtider); Södermöre pastorat newsletter 2019, [svenskakyrkan.se/…](https://svenskakyrkan.se/default.aspx?id=1867145); Landvetter-Härryda, "De liturgiska färgerna", [svenskakyrkan.se/…](https://www.svenskakyrkan.se/landvetter-harryda-pastorat/de-liturgiska-fargerna); unofficial day list: S:t Stefanus koinonia, "Kyrkoårslista" (2015), [stefanuskoinonia.org/…](https://www.stefanuskoinonia.org/wp-content/uploads/2011/11/Kyrko%C3%A5rslista.pdf); read October 2026. | Norway: all of the repo's colors match the 2010 rule (Good Friday violet; the rule allows black as an alternative). Finland: matches the day pages except Maundy Thursday (source violet or blue, repo white) and All Saints (source red, or white at evening services; repo white). Denmark: the church prescribes no colors; the repo follows common custom, but Septuagesima and Sexagesima are purple in the repo where no source was found. Sweden: no national Kyrkohandbok color table was found online; parish pages confirm the seasons, St Stephen red, Maundy Thursday white, Apostles' Day red, St Michael and All Saints white. Trinity Sunday, the Sunday after All Saints, and Domssöndagen vary between sources. See 6-discrepancies.md. |

Escue, "Colors of the Liturgical Seasons" (Lutheran Worship Notes 29, 1994), LCMS Worship, [lcms.org/…](https://www.lcms.org/worship/church-year/colors-of-liturgical-seasons); "How to Prepare the Church Sanctuary for Lent and Holy Week" (from The Altar Guild Manual: LSB Edition, pp. 80-83), CPH blog, [blog.cph.org/…](https://blog.cph.org/worship/preparing-the-church-sanctuary-for-lent-and-holy-week) | The LCMS calendar confirms blue (or violet) Advent; white for Christmas Eve/Day, Epiphany, Transfiguration (both series), All Saints, Trinity and Easter (gold allowed); violet or black for Ash Wednesday; scarlet from Palm Sunday through Maundy Thursday (violet allowed); black Good Friday; red Pentecost and Reformation. The Escue article (an essay from the LW era, not LSB itself) gives green Sundays after Epiphany and after Pentecost, a white Baptism, white minor festivals, and red for martyr festivals and Holy Cross. Disagreements: the LCMS calendar gives Ash Wednesday as "violet or black" (repo has BLACK) and Maundy Thursday as scarlet, "white may be used in some places" (repo has WHITE); the Altar Guild Manual prefers white when Communion is celebrated. No source opened confirms Holy Saturday black over white, Thanksgiving white, or the color of the Last Sunday (Escue suggests gold). LSB itself and the LSB Altar Book could not be read. Applied: Ash Wednesday stays black, which LSB lists first (published source over the website), with violet named in the readout; violet is named as the option for Advent and Holy Week, gold for Easter Day. Still open: Maundy Thursday, where LSB's first color was not confirmed (repo white). |

Escue, "Colors of the Liturgical Seasons" (Lutheran Worship Notes 29, 1994), LCMS Worship, [lcms.org/…](https://www.lcms.org/worship/church-year/colors-of-liturgical-seasons); "How to Prepare the Church Sanctuary for Lent and Holy Week" (from The Altar Guild Manual: LSB Edition, pp. 80-83), CPH blog, [blog.cph.org/…](https://blog.cph.org/worship/preparing-the-church-sanctuary-for-lent-and-holy-week) | The LCMS calendar confirms blue (or violet) Advent; white for Christmas Eve/Day, Epiphany, Transfiguration (both series), All Saints, Trinity and Easter (gold allowed); violet or black for Ash Wednesday; scarlet from Palm Sunday through Maundy Thursday (violet allowed); black Good Friday; red Pentecost and Reformation. The Escue article (an essay from the LW era, not LSB itself) gives green Sundays after Epiphany and after Pentecost, a white Baptism, white minor festivals, and red for martyr festivals and Holy Cross. Disagreements: the LCMS calendar gives Ash Wednesday as "violet or black" (repo has BLACK) and Maundy Thursday as scarlet, "white may be used in some places" (repo has WHITE); the Altar Guild Manual prefers white when Communion is celebrated. No source opened confirms Holy Saturday black over white, Thanksgiving white, or the color of the Last Sunday (Escue suggests gold). LSB itself and the LSB Altar Book could not be read. Applied: Ash Wednesday stays black, which LSB lists first (published source over the website), with violet named in the readout; violet is named as the option for Advent and Holy Week, gold for Easter Day. Still open: Maundy Thursday, where LSB's first color was not confirmed (repo white). |

All match the rules in data/Orthodox/days.csv and periods.csv for 2026. The Liturgical Year page names the Triodion and Pentecostarion as the books of the movable cycle. Ruthenian source only; no Orthodox (OCA/GOARCH) structure page was fetched, though the readings rows already check the Sunday sequence against the OCA lectionary. OCA daily pages confirm the edge cases. When 30 or 31 Dec is a Sunday (31 Dec 2023, 30 Dec 2018), the OCA calls it only 'Sunday after Nativity' and has no 'Sunday before Theophany' that year, which matches the repo's date ge 01-01 rule. When the Nativity falls on a Sunday (2022), the OCA has no Sunday after Nativity: 26 Dec 2022 takes its readings (Gal 1:11-19, Matt 2:13-23) under the Synaxis. Jan 1 2023 is 'Sunday before Theophany'. Sources: [oca.org/…](https://www.oca.org/readings/daily/2023/12/31) ; [oca.org/…](https://www.oca.org/readings/daily/2018/12/30) ; [oca.org/…](https://www.oca.org/readings/daily/2022/12/26) ; [oca.org/…](https://www.oca.org/readings/daily/2023/01/01) |

The UGCC calls this the 'Gregorian style'; it gives the same dates as the Revised Julian until 2800. The decision is for the UGCC in Ukraine; diaspora eparchies decide for themselves. Eastern Orthodox part (which churches use which calendar) not checked against a fetched source. |

The Ruthenian 2026 calendar also matches the Exaltation (14 Sept), the Archangels (8 Nov), the Entry (21 Nov) and the Circumcision with St Basil (1 Jan). One difference: the OCA keeps Great Martyr Katherine on 24 Nov, but the repo has 11-25 (Greek usage). The remaining dates are standard Menaion dates but were not individually fetched. |
| colors | ◐ partial | Slavic color cycle after the Russian Nastolnaya Kniga with Carpatho-Russian (Ruthenian) local customs: gold default, blue Theotokos, dark red/purple Cross, black Lenten weekdays and Holy Week, red fasts and martyrs, green Palm Sunday/Pentecost, white Pascha | "The Liturgical Colors of Vestments in the Orthodox Church", The Catalog of Good Deeds (St Elisabeth Convent, Minsk), 28 Dec 2016, translating Nastol'naya Kniga Sviashchenno-sluzhitelia vol. 4 (Moscow, 1983) via The Messenger of St Andrew's Russian Orthodox Cathedral (Philadelphia, 1999), [catalog.obitel-minsk.com/…](https://catalog.obitel-minsk.com/blog/2016/12/the-liturgical-colors-of-vestments-in); OrthodoxWiki, Liturgical colors, [orthodoxwiki.org/…](https://orthodoxwiki.org/Liturgical_colors) | Confirms the following. Gold when no other color is given. Light blue or white for the Theotokos. Purple or dark red for the Cross (red or green in some places for the Exaltation). Red for martyrs and for the Nativity and Apostles' Fasts. Green for Palm Sunday, Pentecost and the Day of the Holy Spirit. Black for Holy Week. Dark red on Great Thursday. White from the prokeimenon of the Holy Saturday Liturgy. White for Theophany and the Transfiguration. Blue for the Dormition fast and feast (Carpatho-Russian). Pascha: white until the end of Paschal Matins, then bright red through the Paschal season in Russian/Moscow use, but 'In Carpatho-Russian style, in the Paschal season, white, exclusively, is worn'. The repo differs in places: Great Lent is dark color throughout, but the source has 'the weekdays ... black' with dark colors only on Sundays and feasts. The Nativity season is white, but the source has 'Gold is worn from Christmas to Epiphany' (white on Christmas day in some places). See the discrepancies file. The source is a translation hosted by a convent website, not an official OCA page. |

Wikipedia says most Anglican churches use the Roman colors, usually the post-1969 form, with some using the earlier form (black on Good Friday) - which is roughly what the repo does (pre-1969 Roman: violet Septuagesima, red Pentecost octave). For wales-1984 the Church in Wales 2011 scheme directly contradicts the repo (white to Candlemas, green 3 Feb to Shrove Tuesday, green from the Monday after Pentecost): wales-1984 should be split out and sourced from that note. No Anglican Church of Canada guidance on colors (BCP 1962 or BAS) was found online. wales-1984 now follows the 2011 scheme from 2011 on; this row covers it only before then. |

Wikipedia says the Church of England lectionary follows post-1969 Roman usage, which has the same pattern. For the other books (which print no colors) the rule is applied by analogy. The repo already does the St John and Holy Cross exceptions, but the detail text should say so. See discrepancies file for bcp-1962 Conversion of St Paul and wales-1984 Martyrdom of Paul. No Anglican Church of Canada source on the Conversion of St Paul's color was found. GIRM 346(b) (usccb.org) gives red for apostles, evangelists and martyrs in Roman use, which the Anglican schemes follow. Applied: bcp-1962 Conversion of St Paul white; wales-1984 Conversion and Martyrdom of St Paul red from 2011. |

### Ethiopian and Eritrean Orthodox Tewahedo

`oriental-ethiopian` · Oriental Orthodox

| Aspect | Status | What | Source | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| days | ◐ partial | Ethiopian year from Enkutatash (1 Meskerem: 11 Sep, 12 Sep before a Gregorian leap year); Easter by the Alexandrian (Ammonian) reckoning; nine major feasts of the Lord, Meskel 17 Meskerem, Hidar Tsion 21 Hidar, Gizret 6 Tir, Timket 11 Tir, Kana 12 Tir, Megabit Meskel 10 Megabit, Debre Tabor 13 Nehase, Filseta 16 Nehase; fasts (Nineveh, Abiy Tsom, Apostles, Filseta, Tsome Nebiyat) | Ethiopian Orthodox Tewahedo Church, "Religious Holidays and Calendar", [ethiopianorthodox.org/…](https://www.ethiopianorthodox.org/english/calendar.html); WHO Africa, "Ethiopia to celebrate its New Year tomorrow, 12th September" (2019), [afro.who.int/…](https://www.afro.who.int/pt/node/11715); Mahibere Kidusan, "The Fifth Month" (Tir), [eotcmk.org/…](https://eotcmk.org/e/?p=8511); Mahibere Kidusan, "The Seventh Month" (Megabit), [eotcmk.org/…](https://eotcmk.org/e/the-seventh-month/); Mahibere Kidusan, "The Holy Cross and its Celebration", [eotcmk.org/…](https://eotcmk.org/e/the-holy-cross-and-its-celebration/); Mahibere Kidusan, "The Twelfth Month" (Nehase), [eotcmk.org/…](https://eotcmk.org/e/?p=8093); Mahibere Kidusan, "Meskel - Finding of the True Cross", [eotcmk.org/…](https://eotcmk.org/e/?p=3999); Mahibere Kidusan, "The Feast of the Ark of the Covenant" (Hidar 21), [eotcmk.org/…](https://eotcmk.org/e/?p=4049); Mahibere Kidusan, "The Fast of the Prophets (Tsome Nebiyat)", [eotcmk.org/…](https://eotcmk.org/e/the-fast-of-the-prophets-tsome-nebiyat/) and [eotcmk.org/…](https://eotcmk.org/e/?p=3893); Mahibere Kidusan, posts listing page (Debre Zeit as a minor feast), [eotcmk.org/…](https://eotcmk.org/e/page/165/); Brilliant Ethiopia, festivals guide, [brilliant-ethiopia.com/…](https://brilliant-ethiopia.com/guide/festivals); copticchurch.net, "Coptic Feasts 2026", [copticchurch.net/…](https://www.copticchurch.net/calendar/feasts/2026) (shared Alexandrian dates for 2026: Nineveh 2 Feb, Lent 16 Feb, Easter 12 Apr) | Checked: Enkutatash 11 Sep (12 Sep in 2019, matching the repo's leap rule); Meskel 27 Sep with Demera the day before; Hidar Tsion = Hidar 21 (30 Nov); Timket = Tir 11, Kana = Tir 12; Debre Tabor = Nehase 13; Easter by Ammonius; Lent starts on a Monday; Apostles' fast starts after Pentecost. Disagreement: Tsome Nebiyat starts 15 Hidar (24 Nov; 25 Nov in the year before a Gregorian leap year) and is 44 days, but the repo starts it at Genna-43 (25 Nov), a day late in most years. The church's own site gives Abiy Tsom as 56 days (repo: 55 from Monday, plus Zewerede Sunday = 56, so consistent if counted from the Sunday). Not found in a source: Rikbe Kahinat at Easter+24, Feast of the Apostles 5 Hamle (Coptic 5 Epip is verified, same calendar), Ethiopian Nineveh date (matches the Coptic one by the shared rule; unverified for Ethiopia directly). Of the nine minor feasts of the Lord, Gizret (Our Lord and Savior Jesus Christ's Circumcision is celebrated on Tir 6) and Megabit Meskel (Megabit 10 is the founding of the Holy Cross; St Helena found the Cross on Megabit 10 (March 19th)) are added; Lidete Simeon and the Advent Sundays Sibket, Birhan and Nolawi are still left out, as no source found gives their days. Genna is kept on 7 Jan (28 Tahsas in years after a leap year), which julian(12-25) handles correctly. |
| feasts | ◐ partial | Saints' days by Ethiopian date (9 rows): Michael 12 Hidar and 12 Sene, Gabriel 19 Tahsas and 19 Hamle, Aster'eyo Maryam 21 Tir, Kidane Mehret 16 Yekatit, St George 23 Miyazya, Lideta Maryam 1 Ginbot, Tekle Haymanot 24 Nehase | Ethiopian Orthodox Tewahedo Church, "Religious Holidays and Calendar", [ethiopianorthodox.org/…](https://www.ethiopianorthodox.org/english/calendar.html); Mahibere Kidusan, "Aster'eyo Mariam", [eotcmk.org/…](https://eotcmk.org/e/asterio-mariam/); Mahibere Kidusan, "The Fifth Month", [eotcmk.org/…](https://eotcmk.org/e/?p=8511); Mahibere Kidusan, "Feast of the Honored Archangel Saint Michael", [eotcmk.org/…](https://eotcmk.org/e/?p=7001); Mahibere Kidusan, "The Seven Archangels", [eotcmk.org/…](https://eotcmk.org/e/wp-json/wp/v2/posts/6556); Mahibere Kidusan, "Feast of the Great Martyr Saint George", [eotcmk.org/…](https://eotcmk.org/e/feast-of-the-great-martyr-saint-george/); Mahibere Kidusan, "Feasts of Abune Tekle Haimanot", [eotcmk.org/…](https://eotcmk.org/e/feasts-of-abune-tekle-haimanot/) | Dates confirmed: Michael Hedar 12 and Sene 12; Gabriel Tahsas 19 (Hamle given without a day number); Tir 21 = departure of St Mary; Kidane Mehret Yekatit 16; St George 23 Miyazya (1 May); Tekle Haymanot died the 24th of Nehase. Lideta 1 Ginbot only indirectly (church site lists Mary's Nativity as 'May 1'). Name of 21 Tir is wrong in the repo: 'Astemehro' should be 'Aster'eyo Maryam' (also spelled Astereyo/Asterio Mariam). Hamle 19 for Gabriel not confirmed from a source. |
| colors | ~ guess | White feasts, gold between, violet fasts, black Holy Week (project's own scheme) | Searched; no source found prescribing colors. Gnisci, J., "Ecclesiastic dress in Medieval Ethiopia: Preliminary remarks on the visual evidence", in Vryzidis (ed.), The Hidden Life of Textiles in the Medieval and Early Modern Mediterranean (Brepols, 2020), accepted manuscript [discovery-pp.ucl.ac.uk/…](https://discovery-pp.ucl.ac.uk/10111951/1/TextilesAcceptedPaper.pdf) | No source found in which the Ethiopian or Eritrean church prescribes a liturgical color cycle. The only practice source found speaks of 'the colourfulness and rich variety of vestments worn by the Ethiopian clergy' without any seasonal rule. The scheme stays the project's own choice; keep it marked guess. |
| names | ✓ sourced | Ge'ez names of the eight Sundays of the Great Fast: Zewerede, Kidist, Mekurab, Metsagu, Debre Zeit, Gebre Her, Nikodimos, Hosanna; seasons named for the fasts | ethiopianorthodox.org, description of the Lent weeks (PDF), [ethiopianorthodox.org/…](https://ethiopianorthodox.org/amharic/seasonal/lent/descriptionlentweeks.pdf); Mahibere Kidusan, "The Great Lent", [eotcmk.org/…](https://eotcmk.org/e/the-great-lent-2/); Mahibere Kidusan, "The Great Fast", [eotcmk.org/…](https://eotcmk.org/e/the-great-fast/); Mahibere Kidusan, "The Great Fast - Part Two", [eotcmk.org/…](https://eotcmk.org/e/?p=5986); Mahibere Kidusan, posts listing page, [eotcmk.org/…](https://eotcmk.org/e/page/165/) | All eight names and their order match. Spellings vary in the sources (Zewered/Zewerede, Kidist/Kedest, Mikurab/Mekurab, Metsagu/Mestagu, Debre Zeite, Geber hare/Gebre Here, Nicodimus/Niqodimos). Glosses: Zewerede 'He who comes down', Kidist 'Holy', Mekurab 'Temple' (also 'Synagogue/Temple'), Metsagu 'the Paralytic', Gebre Her 'Faithful Servant' (repo 'The Good Servant'; the PDF says 'good servants'). Debre Zeit is a minor feast 'halfway in the fifth week' of Lent, matching Easter-28. Season names (Tsome Nebiyat, Abiy Tsom, Tsome Hawaryat, Tsome Filseta) are the standard fast names; Kiremt (rainy season) and Zemene Yohannes are not checked against a source. |
