It lists the same seven major and seven minor feasts as the repo (minor includes Covenant Thursday and Thomas Sunday). Lent Sunday order matches the Coptic Education list: Preparation week (Matt 6:1-18), 1st Treasures in Heaven, Temptation, Prodigal Son, Samaritan Woman, Paralytic, Man Born Blind, Palm Sunday. Nayrouz = 1 Thout, Cross = 17 Thout and 10 Paremhat (Synaxarium). The parish page says Nayrouz is '12 September on a leap year'; strictly it is the September before a Gregorian leap year (e.g. 2027), which is what the repo computes. In years when 29 Koiak is 8 January the Nativity stays on 7 January (28 Koiak; from memory, unverified); julian(12-25) already does this, but the label '(29 Koiak)' is then off by one. |
| feasts | ✓ sourced | Saints and lesser days by Coptic date (12 rows) | copticchurch.net Synaxarium, [copticchurch.net/…](https://copticchurch.net/synaxarium/)<month>_<day>.html (opened: 1_1, 1_17, 3_12, 3_15, 4_3, 4_29, 5_21, 5_22, 7_10, 7_24, 8_23, 8_30, 9_1, 10_21, 11_5, 12_7, 12_16); copticchurch.net, "Coptic Feasts 2026", [copticchurch.net/…](https://www.copticchurch.net/calendar/feasts/2026) (Fast of the Virgin begins 7 Aug = 1 Mesori) | Every row checked against the copticchurch.net Synaxarium: Michael 12 Hathor, Mina 15 Hathor, Entrance of the Virgin into the Temple 3 Koiak, Dormition 21 Tobi, Anthony 22 Tobi, George 23 Parmouti, Mark 30 Parmouti, Nativity of the Virgin 1 Pashons, Annunciation of the Virgin's birth 7 Mesori, Zeitoun apparition 24 Paremhat, first church of the Virgin at Philippi 21 Paoni. The Virgin's fast begins 1 Mesori (7 Aug 2026). No disagreements. Month names in the repo use academic forms (Thout, Paopi, Hathor, Koiak, Tobi, Meshir, Paremhat, Parmouti, Pashons, Paoni, Epip, Mesori); the church sources use Tout, Baba, Hatour, Kiahk, Toba, Amshir, Baramhat, Baramouda, Bashans, Paona, Abib, Mesra. |
| colors | ◐ partial | White vestments all year (the tonia is always white); in Holy Week the church is hung with black and icons are veiled, replaced by white for the Resurrection | St-Takla.org, "Tonya" (Coptic church terms), [st-takla.org/…](https://st-takla.org/faith/en/terms/tonya.html); Egyptian Streets, "What is Holy Week and how do Copts celebrate it" (9 Apr 2023), [egyptianstreets.com/…](https://egyptianstreets.com/2023/04/09/what-is-holy-week-and-how-do-copts-celebrate-it/) | The church prescribes no cycle of liturgical colors. What the sources describe is practice: the priest's tonia is 'a white tunic worn by all monks and priests while celebrating the Holy Mass', and in Holy Week 'black veils are draped over the walls and along the columns of the church' and icons are covered in black, then replaced with white for the Resurrection. So the black comes from the church's hangings, not the vestments; the repo's wording ('following the vestments', 'vestments ... black in Holy Week') should change. No source found for Covenant Thursday being white. |

Sunday of <month>) | St Bishoy Coptic Orthodox Church, Katameros Sunday Readings for Amshir (PDF), [saintbishoy.ca/…](https://saintbishoy.ca/wp-content/uploads/Katameros_Sunday_Readings_Amshir.pdf) | The Katameros headings read 'Katameros Readings for the First Sunday of Amshir' through 'Fourth Sunday of Amshir', which confirms naming by month. Only Amshir was opened; the 'Sunday of the Little Month' (Nasie) was not confirmed. The repo's month spellings are academic forms, not the ones the Katameros uses (Amshir, not Meshir). No edition was named before; this is a parish-published English Katameros. |

Fasts: the Syriac Orthodox Church keeps the Apostles' fast for three days from 26 June and the Virgin's fast for five days from 10 August (Barsoum; BSM, a Syriac Orthodox cathedral in India; SOR). The Malankara Orthodox keep 16-29 June and 1-15 August (KL, adapted from mosc.in). periods.csv applies the Malankara dates to both variants, so it is wrong for 'syriac'. Possible movable/fixed mismatches: the NE American Malankara diocese lists Severus on 8 Feb, Barsaumo on 3 Feb and Ephrem on 24 Feb as fixed dates, while days.csv makes them movable (easter-66, easter+46, easter-43). See 4-discrepancies.md. |

That is the same day as the repo rule sun<12-25-49. The Malankara diocese lists Koodhosh Eetho on 2 Nov and Hoodhosh Eetho on 9 Nov for 2025, which agrees. The Sunday sequence matches. Fasts: the Syriac Orthodox Church keeps the Apostles' fast for three days from 26 June and the Virgin's fast for five days from 10 August (Barsoum; BSM, a Syriac Orthodox cathedral in India; SOR). The Malankara Orthodox keep 16-29 June and 1-15 August (KL, adapted from mosc.in). periods.csv applies the Malankara dates to both variants, so it is wrong for 'syriac'. Possible movable/fixed mismatches: the NE American Malankara diocese lists Severus on 8 Feb, Barsaumo on 3 Feb and Ephrem on 24 Feb as fixed dates, while days.csv makes them movable (easter-66, easter+46, easter-43). See 4-discrepancies.md. |

Skaria: in 1955, immovable feasts moved to the Gregorian calendar, except in the Holy Land. soc-wus: "The Julian calendar continues to determine the observation of the Great Lent and Week of Passion." The Church of the East keeps the Cross on 13 September, not the 14th: Andrious says "precisely on the 13th of September," the ACOE 2024 calendar and the lectionary both have 13 Sep, and so does Maclean. days.csv has sliba on 09-14. If the Cross is 13 Sep, the eliya rule sun<=09-13 should be sun<=09-12. The two rules differ only when 13 Sep is a Sunday and Easter is late, but then the repo puts Eliya one week late. |
